155Q = Computer assignment number for JAL #1628.
RB = Reinforced Beacon return (Normal)
•
KI = Primary radar return, uncorrelated (Skin/surface)
BT = Secondary radar return, (Beacon/transponder)
Number of pages in computer printout = 15
Pages with uncorrelated returns: 2,3,4,5,6,7, 10.
19 = NUMBER OF UNCORRELATED RETURNS
86 = NUMBER OF USABLE RADAR RETURNS
105 = TOTAL NUMBER OF RETURNS FOR ABOVE TIME FRAME.

0253:13,

===== PAGE 19 =====
{"alpha_words": 105, "chars": 724, "method": "ocr", "page": 19, "variant": "plain-vision"}
UNCURRELATED RADAR SIGYALS
An "uncorrelated primary and beacon (secondary) return on a radar
screen occurs when the tadar energy that is sent up toward the aircraft
(primary signal) returns off the surface of the aircraft at a slightly
different moment that the beacon (secondary) transponder signal and the
two do not match up as being at the same place or same computer radar
cell.
--RADAR COMPUTER CELL, 1/4 MILE--
RADAR CELL
MILE--
SAME AIRCRAFT
(Crossing Cell)
-- RADAR
CELL,
SECONDARY
RADAR
RETURN
(Beacon)
(Transponder)

===== PAGE 20 =====
{"alpha_words": 155, "chars": 1009, "method": "ocr", "page": 20, "variant": "plain-vision"}
Memorandum
US Department
of Transportation
Federal Aviation
Administration
Subject: ACTION: Description of Radar Split Image
Date:
February 5, 1987
From: PUBLIC AFFAIRS OFFICER, AAL-5
Manager, Always Facilities, AAL-400
Reply t
Attri. o
To:
The Alaskan Region FAA Air Iraffic Division has informed me that the'
intermitent radar images of Japan Air Lines fl1ght 1628, on November 17,
1986, which occured between It. Yukon and Anchorage, are the result of
"split images".
Do you concur with this interpretation? If you do, please provide me
with an easy to understand written description of what a "split Image"
is, how It occurs, approximately how often It occurs, and why It might
have, or did occur on this flight segment of this flight.
Your response will be used to provide the news media and the public with
an explanation of what a "split image" Is and how it relates to this
flight.
I will need this Information for media response by February 18, 1987.
Thank you.
Vail
Paul Steucke
cc:
3A852 12003,500

UNCORRELATED RADAR SIGNALS
An "uncorrelated primary and beacon (secondary) return on a radar
screen occurs when the radar energy that is sent up toward the aircraft
(prinary signal) returns off the surface of the aircraft at a slightly
different moment that the beacon (secondary) transponder signal and the
two do not match up as being at the same place or same computer radar
cell.
--RADAR COMPUTER CELL, 1/4 MILE--

SAME AIRCRAFT
(Crossing Cell)
